{\mathfrak{a}}=(a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3}\nu_{3})
.
We justify this in a similar way as in
[
14
, Proof of Lemma 5.2 assuming Proposition 5.1 pp. 13-14]
.

If
\alpha=\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i}
and
\beta=\sum_{i=1}b_{i}\nu_{i}
with
{\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathcal{X}}
are such that
(\alpha)=(\beta)

then
\beta\alpha^{-1}
is a unit of
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
.
But
|\sigma(\alpha)|\ll X
for all embedding
\sigma
and since
\alpha=N(\alpha)\prod_{\sigma\not=Id}\sigma(\alpha)^{-1}
we have

|a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3}\nu_{3}|\gg\eta_{1}^{1/10}X
by (
4.4
)
and then

\frac{\beta}{\alpha}=1+\frac{\beta-\alpha}{\alpha}=1+O(\eta_{1}^{9/10}).

If
\alpha\not=\beta
then
\beta\alpha^{-1}
can’t be a unit because the length between two units is
\gg 1
and we have a contradiction.

Next we consider the sets

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)=\Big\{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}:{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\ \text{and}\ p|f(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3})\Big\}

and for any ideal
{\mathfrak{d}}
,

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathfrak{d}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)=\{{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p):{\mathfrak{d}}|{\mathfrak{a}}\}.

Let
N_{0}^{4}=\min_{(\alpha)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}}N(\alpha)
.
Let
\eta_{\mathcal{X}}
and
\eta_{2}
defined by

\eta_{\mathcal{X}}=\frac{1}{(\log X)^{A}},\qquad\eta_{2}=\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{10000\ell^{2}}.

(9.11)

By the definition of
{\mathcal{X}}
,
N(a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3})\in[N_{0}^{4},N_{0}^{4}(1+O(\eta_{1}))]
for all

(a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
. We can choose
O(\eta_{2}^{-1}\eta_{1})
reals
X_{0}
with
X_{0}^{4}\in[N_{0}^{4},N_{0}^{4}(1+O(\eta_{1}))]
so that
the sets

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)=\Big\{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i}\big)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p):N\big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i}\big)\in[X_{0}^{4},X_{0}^{4}+\eta_{2}X_{0}^{4}[\Big\},

form a partition of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)
.
Next we introduce the sets

\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{\mathfrak{d}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)=\Big\{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i}\big)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p):{\mathfrak{d}}|\big(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i}\big)\Big\}.

By (
4.9
), there exists
\varepsilon>0
such that
X^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\theta_{i}+\min(\theta_{0},\ldots,\theta_{\ell})}>X^{4+\varepsilon}
and by (
4.7
) the intervals
[X^{\theta_{i}},X^{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}]
do not overlap. Thus for each
{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}

such that
N({\mathfrak{a}})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell})
with

X^{\theta_{i}}\leq q_{i}\leq X^{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}
, is divisible by exactly one prime ideal
{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}
with

N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i})\in[X^{\theta_{i}},X^{\theta^{\prime}_{i}}]
(for all
1\leq i\leq\ell
).

We are now ready to settle the connection between the set
{\mathcal{A}}_{q}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)
in Theorem
4.1
and the sets of ideals just defined above. For any primes
q_{1},\ldots,q_{\ell}
with
q_{i}\in[X^{\theta_{i}},X^{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}]
, we have

|{\mathcal{A}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i})=q_{i}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}_{1}\cdots{\mathfrak{P}}_{\ell}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|.

(9.12)

Any ideal
(a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3}\nu_{3})
counted in (
9.12
) may be factored as

(a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3}\nu_{3})={\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}{\mathfrak{P}}_{i},

(9.13)

where each
{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}
is a prime ideal with norm in
[X^{\theta_{i}},X^{\theta^{\prime}_{i}}]
and
{\mathfrak{I}}
is an ideal with

N({\mathfrak{I}})\in{\mathcal{I}}_{0}:=\Bigl[\frac{X_{0}^{4}X^{-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\theta_{i}^{\prime}}}{m_{0}},\frac{X_{0}^{4}(1+\eta_{2})X^{-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\theta_{i}}}{m_{0}}\Bigr]=[I_{1},I_{2}],

(9.14)

say.

We choose now
O(\eta_{2}^{-1}\log X)
reals
I\in{\mathcal{I}}_{0}
such that
{\mathcal{I}}_{0}
is covered by
the union of the intervals
[I,I(1+\eta_{2})[
. Let
\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{0}
denote the set of these reals
I
.

Since we have
(N(\sum_{i=1}^{3}a_{i}\nu_{i})/m_{0},m)=1
when
{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})
, we have

(m^{\prime},N({\mathfrak{I}})\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i}))=1
.

For brevity we will write
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)
in place of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)
when the context will be clear.

To have a precise control of the size of the norms of some ideals,
we cover each interval
[\theta_{i},\theta^{\prime}_{i}]
by
O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{-2})
distinct intervals of size
O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2})

so that,

\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta^{\prime}_{i}]=\cup_{{\mathbf{l}}\in E}{\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}}),

(9.15)

where
E
is some subset of
\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
and each
{\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}})
is of type
{\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}})=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[t_{i},t^{\prime}_{i})

with
|t_{i}^{\prime}-t_{i}|\ll\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}
(except that in the intervals with
t^{\prime}_{i}=\theta_{i}^{\prime}
we take the whole segment
[t_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]
), (cf
[
14
, section 8 p.45]
).

We write
{\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}})=\prod{\mathcal{R}}_{1}({\mathbf{l}})\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}({\mathbf{l}})
with
{\mathcal{R}}_{2}({\mathbf{l}})
representing the first
\ell^{\prime}
coordinates and
{\mathcal{R}}_{1}({\mathbf{l}})
the final
\ell-\ell^{\prime}
coordinates.

For a polytope
{\mathcal{R}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
(for some
s
), we define

\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{R}}({\mathfrak{a}})=\begin{cases}1,&{\mathfrak{a}}={\mathfrak{p}}_{1}\cdots{\mathfrak{p}}_{s}\ {\rm with}\ N({\mathfrak{p}}_{i})=X^{e_{i}}\ {\rm and}\ (e_{1},\ldots,e_{s})\in{\mathcal{R}},\\
0,&{\rm otherwise.}\end{cases}

Thus we need to study the quantity

T({\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}})):=\sum_{X^{\tau}\leq p\leq X^{\tau^{\prime}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}\in{\mathcal{A}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}({\mathbf{l}})}({\mathfrak{a}}),

(9.16)

with
{\mathcal{I}}:=\{{\mathfrak{I}}:N({\mathfrak{I}})\in[I,I+\eta_{2}I[,(N({\mathfrak{I}}),m)=1\}
.

9.6
Approximation weights

We recall that
\eta_{2}=\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{10000\ell^{2}}
.
A key idea of
[
14
]
is to approximate the indicator
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}
by a weight
\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}

which will be more easy to control.
For
{\mathcal{S}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{s}
we consider the function

c_{\mathcal{S}}(t)=\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}{(e_{1},\ldots,e_{s})\in{\mathcal{S}}}\\
{\sum_{i=1}^{s}e_{i}\in I_{t}}\end{subarray}}\frac{de_{1}\cdots de_{s}}{\eta_{2}^{1/2}\prod_{i=1}^{s}e_{i}},

(9.17)

{\rm where}\ I_{t}:=\Big[\frac{\log t}{\log X},\frac{\log(t+\eta_{2}^{1/2}t)}{\log X}\Big].

In this previous definition we have
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}e_{i}\in I_{t}
if and only if
X^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}e_{i}}\in[t,t(1+\sqrt{\eta_{2}}]
.
This function is so that
c_{\mathcal{S}}(N({\mathfrak{a}}))
corresponds to the probability for an ideal of norm close to
N({\mathfrak{a}})
to
have a prime factorisation compatible with
{\mathcal{S}}
(cf.
[
14
, section 8]
).
We recall below some properties of this function that we will frequently use later on.

Lemma 9.12
.

•

If
{\mathcal{S}}=\prod_{i=1}^{s}[s_{i},s^{\prime}_{i}]
is an hyperrectangle with
\min s_{i}>\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
\ell>1
, then

c_{\mathcal{S}}(t+\delta)-c_{\mathcal{S}}(t)\ll\frac{\delta}{t}

•

If
{\mathcal{S}}=\prod_{i=1}^{s}[s_{i},s^{\prime}_{i}]
is an hyperrectangle with
\min s_{i}>\varepsilon_{0}>0
then

c_{\mathcal{S}}(t)\ll_{\varepsilon_{0}}\frac{1}{\log X}.

Proof.

The first part is a particular case of
[
14
, Lemma 8.3 (iii)]
.
The proof of the second point is a direct computation analogous to
[
14
]
:

c_{\mathcal{S}}(t)\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\eta_{2}}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}{e_{i}\in[s_{i},s_{i}^{\prime}]}\\
{1\leq i\leq s-1}\end{subarray}}\Big[\int_{e_{s}\in I_{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{s-1}e_{i}}\frac{{\mathrm{\,d\,}}e_{s}}{s_{s}}\Big]\prod_{i=1}^{s-1}\frac{{\mathrm{\,d\,}}e_{i}}{s_{i}}.

The integral over
e_{\ell}
is
O(\sqrt{\eta_{2}}(\log X)^{-1})
and the contribution of the other integrals is
O(1)
.
∎

Let
\varepsilon_{00}>0
and
R:=X^{\varepsilon_{00}}
. The approximate weights of
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}
are defined by

\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}}):=c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(N({\mathfrak{b}}))\sum_{{\mathfrak{d}}|{\mathfrak{b}}}\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}},

(9.18)

where

\lambda_{\delta}:=\begin{cases}\mu({\mathfrak{d}})\log\frac{R}{N({\mathfrak{d}})},&N({\mathfrak{d}})<R,\\
0,&{\rm otherwise.}\end{cases}

Remark
.

Our weights are somewhat simpler than the one introduced in
[
14
]
, because we don’t need to take care of the perturbations caused by a possible exceptional character
\chi^{*}
. (Ultimately we will only require estimates with moduli up to a fixed power of
\log{X}
, whereas in
[
14
]
larger moduli needed to be considered due to losses occurring in high dimensions.)

We now write

T({\mathcal{R}})=T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})+T_{1}({\mathcal{R}}),

where

\displaystyle T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}}),

(9.19)

\displaystyle T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})-\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})),

(9.20)

and

P_{1}:=X^{\tau},\qquad P_{2}:=X^{\tau^{\prime}}.

For brevity again we will write
T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})
and
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
in place of
T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}},{\mathbf{v}}_{0})
and
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}},{\mathbf{v}}_{0})
when
{\mathbf{v}}_{0}
is clear from the context. We see that Theorem
4.1
follows immediately from the following two propositions.

Proposition 9.13

(Estimate for
T_{sieve}
)
.

If we have

\epsilon_{00}<\sum_{j=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\theta_{j}-1-12\tau^{\prime},

then

\displaystyle T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})=(2+O(\eta_{2}^{1/2}))\log({1+\eta_{2}})|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI)\frac{g(m^{\prime})}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}\frac{\log(P_{2}/P_{1})}{\varphi(D_{f})},

with

\displaystyle g(m^{\prime})=\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}|(m^{\prime})}\Big(1-\frac{\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Big)^{-1}.

Proposition 9.14

(Bound for
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
and
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
be as above. If we have

\tau^{\prime}<\min\Bigl(\frac{4-2\theta^{\prime}_{1}-\ldots-2\theta^{\prime}_{\ell^{\prime}}}{100},\frac{\theta_{1}+\cdots+\theta_{\ell^{\prime}}-1}{100}\Bigr),

then for any
A>0
we have

T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})\ll_{A}\frac{|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|}{(\log{X})^{A}}.

We remark that we are assumming the general setup in Propositions
9.13
and
9.14
; in particular, the constants
\theta_{1},\theta_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\theta_{\ell},\theta_{\ell}^{\prime}
determining
{\mathcal{R}}
are assumed to satisfy (
4.6
)-(
4.10
).

We will establish Proposition
9.13
in Section
10
and the harder Proposition
9.14
in Section
11
. The presence of the sum over primes
p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]
introduces few additional complications to
T_{sieve}
and Proposition
9.13
, but quite significant additional technical details to
T_{1}
and Proposition
9.14
. Assuming these propositions for now, we can establish Theorem
4.1
by putting all our manipulations together.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
assuming Propositions
9.13
and
9.14
.

We recall from (
9.8
) and (
9.10
)
that

{\mathcal{A}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},m,p)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{0}|m^{\infty}\\
m_{0}\leq D_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|+O\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{1}^{3}\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}}{m^{2}D_{0}^{1/2}}\Bigr).

(9.21)

We concetrate on the
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
terms. We recall from (
9.12
), (
9.14
) and (
9.16
) that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i})=q_{i}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{P}}_{1}\cdots{\mathfrak{P}}_{\ell}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|

\displaystyle=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{I}}\\
\sqcup{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i})=q_{i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathfrak{J}}\\
N({\mathfrak{J}})\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{J}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}\in\tilde{A}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{I}}\\
\sqcup{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{R}}_{1},{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]=\sqcup{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathfrak{J}}\\
N({\mathfrak{J}})\in{\mathcal{I}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathfrak{b}}\\
{\mathfrak{J}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}\in\tilde{A}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}}).

By assumption of Theorem
4.1
, we have
\tau^{\prime}<(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\theta_{i}-1)/100
, and so choosing
\epsilon_{00}
sufficiently small means that the hypothesis of Proposition
9.13
is satisfied. Thus, summing over
p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]
and applying Propositions
9.13
and
9.14
(with a suitably large constant
A
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{I}}\\
\sqcup{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]=\sqcup{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\end{subarray}}\Bigl(T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})+T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})\Bigr)

\displaystyle=(2+O(\eta_{2}^{1/2}))\eta_{2}\frac{\log(P_{2}/P_{1})}{\phi(D_{f})}\frac{g(m^{\prime})}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}T_{3}+O\Bigl(\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}}{(\log{X})^{A-O(1)}}\Bigr),

(9.22)

where

T_{3}:=\sum_{X_{0}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{I}}\\
\sqcup{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]=\sqcup{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\end{subarray}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|c_{{\mathcal{R}}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI).

Here we used that there are at most
O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{-2})
subsets
{\mathcal{R}}
,
O(\eta_{2}^{-1}\eta_{1})
reals
X_{0}
and
O(\eta_{2}^{-1}\log X)
reals
I
to bound the contribution from
T_{1}
by Proposition
9.14
.

We now concentrate on
T_{3}
. Since the subsets
{\mathcal{R}}
form a partition of
{\mathcal{T}}:=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]
, we find

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\\
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]=\sqcup{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}\end{subarray}}c_{{\mathcal{R}}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI)=c_{\mathcal{T}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI),

so

T_{3}=\sum_{X_{0}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{I}}\\
\sqcup{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}_{0}\end{subarray}}c_{\mathcal{T}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI).

By Lemma
9.12
applied to
c_{\mathcal{T}}
we have for all
I\in{\mathcal{I}}_{0}

\begin{split}c_{\mathcal{T}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI)&=\frac{1}{\eta_{2}}\int_{I}^{I(1+\eta_{2})}\frac{c_{\mathcal{T}}(X_{0}^{4}/mv)}{v}dv+O(\eta_{2}).\\
\end{split}

Expanding the definition (
9.17
) of
c_{{\mathcal{T}}}
and swapping the order of summation and integration, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{0}}\int_{I}^{I(1+\eta_{2})}

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\mathcal{T}}(X_{0}^{4}/mv)}{v}dv

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta_{2}^{1/2}}\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}{e_{i}\in[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]}\\
{1\leq i\leq\ell}\end{subarray}}\sum_{I\in\hat{\mathcal{I}}_{0}}\int\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in[I,I(1+\eta_{2})[\\
v\in\Big[\frac{X_{0}^{4}}{m\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}X^{e_{i}}},\frac{X_{0}^{4}(1+\sqrt{\eta_{2}})}{m\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}X^{e_{i}}}\Big]\end{subarray}}\frac{dv}{v}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{{\mathrm{\,d\,}}e_{i}}{e_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\eta_{2}^{1/2}}\iint\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}e_{i}\in[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\prime}]\\
1\leq i\leq\ell\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\int_{X_{0}^{4}/(m\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}X^{e_{i}})}^{X_{0}^{4}(1+\sqrt{\eta_{2}})/(m\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}X^{e_{i}})}\frac{dv}{v}\Bigr)\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\frac{{\mathrm{\,d\,}}e_{i}}{e_{i}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(1+\sqrt{\eta_{2}})}{\eta_{2}^{1/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\log\Big(\frac{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}{\theta_{i}}\Big).

We note that this is independent of
X_{0}
, so we find

\displaystyle T_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{\log(1+\sqrt{\eta_{2}})}{\eta_{2}^{3/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\log\Big(\frac{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}{\theta_{i}}\Big)\sum_{X_{0}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|+O\Bigl(\log{X}\sum_{X_{0}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1+O(\sqrt{\eta_{2}}))}{\eta_{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\log\Big(\frac{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}{\theta_{i}}\Big)|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X)|.

(9.23)

Putting together (
9.21
), (
9.22
) and (
9.23
) we find

\displaystyle\sum_{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}q_{1},\dots q_{\ell}\\
q_{i}\in[X^{\theta_{i}},X^{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}]\end{subarray}}{\mathcal{A}}_{q_{1}\cdots q_{\ell}}({\mathbf{u}}_{0},m,p)

\displaystyle=2\frac{\log{\frac{P_{2}}{P_{1}}}}{\phi(D_{f})}\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\log\Big(\frac{\theta_{i}^{\prime}}{\theta_{i}}\Big)|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X)|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{0}|m^{\infty}\\
m_{0}\leq D_{0}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})}\frac{g(m^{\prime})}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}

\displaystyle\qquad+O\Bigl(\eta_{2}^{1/2}\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}\Bigr)+O\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{1}^{3}\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}}{D_{0}^{1/2}}\Bigr).

(9.24)

Finally it remains to estimate the inner double sum. The summand is independent of
{\mathbf{v}}_{0}
, so recalling that
m^{\prime}=m_{0}m
we are left to estimate

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{m_{0}<D_{0}}\\
{m_{0}|m^{\infty}}\end{subarray}}\frac{|{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})|}{(mm_{0})^{3}}\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}|(m)}\Big(\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}\frac{\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}}^{k})}{N({\mathfrak{P}}^{k})}\Big)^{-1}.

(9.25)

By (
9.7
),

|{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})|\leq m_{0}^{2}m,

and thus for any given
m

the sum over
m_{0}
converges. We may therefore extend it to all
m_{0}\geq 1
cost of an admissible error term.
Next we note that the sets of the
{\mathbf{a}}\in[1,X]^{3}

with
{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{\mathbf{u}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m)
can be partitioned into sets of the
{\mathbf{a}}\in[1,X]^{3}
such that
{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ mm_{0})
, with
m_{0}\leq X^{2}
and
{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{M}}({\mathbf{u}}_{0})
, and so

\begin{split}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{m_{0}<D_{0}}\\
{m_{0}|m^{\infty}}\end{subarray}}\frac{|{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})|}{(mm_{0})^{3}}&=(1+O(D_{0}^{-1/4}))\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{m_{0}<X^{2}}\\
{m_{0}|m^{\infty}}\end{subarray}}\frac{|{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})|}{(mm_{0})^{3}}\\
&=\frac{(1+O(D_{0}^{-1/4})}{X^{3}+O(X^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{m_{0}<X^{2}}\\
{m_{0}|m^{\infty}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{M}}(m_{0})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{a}}\in[1,X]^{3}}\\
{{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{{\mathbf{v}}_{0}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m_{0})}\end{subarray}}1\\
&=\frac{(1+O(D_{0}^{-1/4}))}{X^{3}+O(X^{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{a}}\in[1,X]^{3}}\\
{{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{{\mathbf{u}}_{0}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m_{0})}\end{subarray}}1\\
&=\frac{1}{m^{3}}(1+O(D_{0}^{-1/4})).\end{split}

Substituting this into (
9.24
) and recalling
D_{0}=\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{-2}\eta_{1}^{-1}\gg(\log{X})^{2A}
,
\eta_{2}\ll(\log{X})^{-2A}
and
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X)|=\eta_{1}^{3}X_{1}X_{2}X_{3}+O(\eta_{2}X_{1}X_{2}X_{3})
gives Theorem
4.1
.
∎

10
Proposition
9.13
: The term
T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})
.

In this part we obtain an analogue of
[
14
, Lemma 8.6]
by expanding the sieve terms and applying Proposition
9.11
.

If
{\mathfrak{a}}
and
{\mathfrak{b}}
are some ideals satisfying
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})=\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})=1
then
{\mathfrak{a}}
and
{\mathfrak{b}}

factor into prime ideals as
{\mathfrak{a}}=\prod_{i=\ell^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}
,
{\mathfrak{b}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{P}}_{i}

with
N({\mathfrak{P}}_{i})\in[X^{t_{i}},X^{t_{i}}(1+O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}\log X))]
for
1\leq i\leq\ell
.
In particular,

N({\mathfrak{a}})\in[A^{4},A^{4}(1+O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}\log X))],\quad N({\mathfrak{b}})\in[B^{4},B^{4}(1+O(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}\log X))]

where

A^{4}:=X^{\sum_{i=\ell^{\prime}+1}^{\ell}t_{i}},\quad B^{4}:=X^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}t_{i}}.

Lemma 10.1
.

Let
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}R
and
{\mathcal{R}}={\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}
. Then we have

T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})=M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})+E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

where
M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
is given by

\displaystyle M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}\\
{N({\mathfrak{d}}),m)=1}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}\Big(\frac{X_{0}^{4}}{m_{0}N({\mathbf{a}}{\mathfrak{I}})}\Big)|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}}),m^{\prime},p)|,

and
E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
satisfies

\sum_{{\mathcal{R}}}\sum_{X_{0}}|E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})|\ll\eta_{2}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{-2\ell}(\log X)^{11}\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}.

Proof.

We substitute our definition (
9.18
) of
\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}
into our expression (
9.19
) for
T_{sieve}
, and write
{\mathfrak{u}}={\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}
. This gives

\begin{split}T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\sum_{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathfrak{u}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)}\\
{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}|{\mathfrak{u}}}\\
\end{subarray}}c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(N({\mathfrak{u}}/{\mathbf{a}}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0})).\end{split}

(10.1)

If
{\mathfrak{u}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)
then
N({\mathfrak{u}})\in[X_{0}^{4},X_{0}^{4}(1+\eta_{2})]
. By Lemma
9.12
, this implies

c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(N({\mathfrak{u}}/{\mathbf{a}}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}))=c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/m_{0}N({\mathbf{a}}{\mathfrak{I}}))+O(\eta_{2}).
Thus we write

T_{sieve}({\mathcal{R}})=M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})+O(E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})),

(10.2)

where
M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
is as given in the Lemma and

\displaystyle E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle:=\eta_{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\sum_{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}|\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}||\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|.

(10.3)

We concentrate on
E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
. For any
(\sum_{i=1}^{3}x_{i}\nu_{i})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}
, the number of primes
p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]
such that
p|f(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})
is finite. This allows us to remove the summation over
p
and replace
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|
with
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},1)|
in
E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
at the cost of a factor
O(1)
.

We then apply Proposition
9.11
to estimate
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},1)|
, recalling that
N({\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{M}}_{0})\ll X^{4}R/B^{4}
and
m^{\prime}\ll(\log X)^{O(1)}
. This gives

\begin{split}E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})&\ll\eta_{2}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{a}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}\\
{(N({\mathfrak{d}}),m^{\prime})=1}\end{subarray}}|\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}|\frac{|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathcal{X}})|\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})}{N({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{d}})(m^{\prime})^{3}}\\
&+X^{o(1)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}\\
{(N({\mathfrak{d}}),m)=1}\end{subarray}}|\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}|\Bigl(X^{2}\Bigl(\frac{X^{4}R}{B^{4}}\Bigr)^{1/3}+\frac{X^{4}R}{B^{4}}X\Bigr).\end{split}

(10.4)

Crudely, if
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}R
, we see the second term in (
10.4
) contributes to (
10.4
)

\ll X^{3+o(1)}\Bigl(\frac{XR^{4}}{B^{4}}+\Bigl(\frac{XR^{4}}{B^{4}}\Bigr)^{1/3}\Bigr)\ll X^{3-\epsilon/4}.

(10.5)

By an Euler product upper bound and Lemma
9.6
, we see that the first term contributes to (
10.4
)

\displaystyle\ll\eta_{2}(\log{X})|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime})|\sum_{N({\mathfrak{I}}),N({\mathfrak{a}}),N({\mathfrak{d}})<X}\frac{|\rho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})|}{N({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})}

\displaystyle\ll\eta_{2}(\log{X})^{9}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime})|.

(10.6)

Thus, substituting (
10.5
) and (
10.6
) into (
10.4
) we find for
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}R

E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})\ll\eta_{2}(\log{X})^{9}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime})|+X^{3-\epsilon/4}.

Summing this over all hyperrectangles
{\mathcal{R}}
and all relevant
X_{0}
, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathcal{R}}}\sum_{X_{0}}E_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle\ll\eta_{2}(\log{X})^{9}\sum_{{\mathcal{R}}}\sum_{X_{0}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime})|+X^{3-\epsilon/5}.

\displaystyle\ll\eta_{2}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{-2\ell}(\log{X})^{4}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime})|+X^{3-\epsilon/5}

\displaystyle\ll\eta_{2}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{-2\ell}(\log X)^{11}\prod_{i=1}^{3}X_{i}+X^{3-\epsilon/5}.

(10.7)

This gives the result.
∎

Thus we have to evaluate
M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Let
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}RP_{2}^{12}
and let
M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
be as given by Lemma
10.1
. Then we have

M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})=(2+O(\eta_{2}^{1/2}))(\log{1+\eta_{2}})|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI)\frac{g(m^{\prime})}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}\frac{\log(P_{2}/P_{1})}{\varphi(D_{f})}.

Proof.

First we want to apply Proposition
9.11
to estimate
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{J}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|
. To do this we split according to residue classes
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. For any
(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})
such that
f(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
let
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}})
be a solution of the two equations
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}})\equiv{\mathbf{y}}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
and
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}})\equiv{\mathbf{v}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})
. Thus

|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)|=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
f(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}}}(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}}),pm^{\prime},1)|

We recall that
p\leq P_{2}
and
N({\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathrm{\,d\,}})\ll XR/B
. Therefore, by Proposition
9.11
, we can replace
|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}_{{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}}}(\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{0}({\mathbf{y}}),pm^{\prime},1)|
with
\rho_{{\mathbf{v}}}({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})|{\mathcal{A}}(X_{0},\chi)|/p^{3}m^{\prime}{}^{3}N({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})
in
M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
at the cost of a term bounded by

\sum_{p\leq P_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
f(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\Bigl(X^{2+o(1)}\Bigl(\frac{XR}{B^{4}}\Bigr)^{1/3}P_{2}+X^{o(1)}\frac{XR}{B^{4}}P_{2}^{4}\Bigr).

This is
O(X^{3-\epsilon/4})
provided
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}RP_{2}^{12}
.

Since the function
\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}
is multiplicative,
({\mathfrak{a}},{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})=1
, and
{\mathfrak{a}}
is a product of degree one prime ideals of large enough norm, by Lemma
9.4
, we have

\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})/N({\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})=\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})/(N({\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})N({\mathfrak{a}}))
. Thus

M_{1}({\mathcal{R}})=M_{2}({\mathcal{R}})+O(X^{3-\epsilon/4}),

(10.8)

where

\displaystyle M_{2}({\mathcal{R}})

\displaystyle:=|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0},{\mathcal{X}})|\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\frac{n_{p}}{p}\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})\Bigr),

(10.9)

\displaystyle Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\mathfrak{a}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})\frac{c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/(m_{0}N({\mathbf{a}}{\mathfrak{I}})))}{N({\mathfrak{a}})},

(10.10)

\displaystyle Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{N({\mathfrak{d}})<R}\\
{(N({\mathfrak{d}}),m^{\prime})=1}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{\mathfrak{d}}\frac{\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})}{N({\mathfrak{d}}{\mathfrak{I}})(m^{\prime})^{3}}

(10.11)

\displaystyle n_{p}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{p^{2}}|\{y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p):\,f(y_{1},y_{2},y_{3})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\}|.

(10.12)

First we simplify
Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})
. Since this is a sum of a smooth function over products of
\ell
prime ideals in a bounded region, this can be estimated using the Prime Ideal Theorem. Following the arguments of
[
14
, Section 8, proof of Lemma 8.6]
we find that

Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})=c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/m_{0}N({\mathfrak{I}}))+O(\eta_{2}).

We recall that
{\mathcal{I}}=\{{\mathfrak{I}}:(N({\mathfrak{I}}),m)=1,\,N({\mathfrak{I}})\in[I,I+\eta_{2}I]\}
.

Thus, by Lemma
9.12
, we have

Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})=c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/m_{0}I)+O(\eta_{2}).

(10.13)

Now we consider
Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})
. By Lemma
9.5
we find that

Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})=\frac{h({\mathfrak{I}})g((m^{\prime}))\tilde{\mathfrak{S}}}{\gamma_{K}m^{\prime}{}^{3}}+O(16^{\omega((m){\mathfrak{I}})}\exp(-c\sqrt{\log R})),

(10.14)

where

\displaystyle g((m))

\displaystyle:=\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}|(m)}\Big(1-\frac{\varrho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Big)^{-1},

\displaystyle h({\mathfrak{J}})

\displaystyle:=\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}|{\mathfrak{J}}}\Bigl(1-\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)^{-1}\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}_{2}^{e}||{\mathfrak{J}}}\Bigl(\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}}^{e})}{N({\mathfrak{P}}^{e})}-\frac{\rho_{\mathbf{v}}({\mathfrak{P}}^{e+1})}{N({\mathfrak{P}}^{e+1})}\Bigr).

Putting together (
10.13
) and (
10.14
), we see that

\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})=\frac{g((m^{\prime}))\tilde{\mathfrak{S}}c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/m_{0}I)}{\gamma_{K}m^{\prime}{}^{3}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{J}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}h({\mathfrak{J}})+O(\eta_{2}^{2}I).

(10.15)

Since
h({\mathfrak{I}})
is multiplicative, the sum can be calculated by a contour computation

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mathfrak{J}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}h({\mathfrak{J}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{2-i\infty}^{2+i\infty}\frac{I^{s}((1+\eta_{2})^{s}-1)}{s}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}}\frac{h({\mathfrak{I}})}{N({\mathfrak{I}})^{s}}ds

\displaystyle=\text{Res}_{s=0}\Bigl(\frac{I^{s}((1+\eta_{2})^{s}-1)}{s}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}}\frac{h({\mathfrak{I}})}{N({\mathfrak{I}})^{s}}\Bigr)+O(\exp(-c\sqrt{\log{R}})).

(10.16)

We see that the residue is given by

\displaystyle\gamma_{K}\log(1+\eta_{2})\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}}\Bigl(1+h({\mathfrak{P}})+h({\mathfrak{P}}^{2})+...\Bigr)\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{K}\log(1+\eta_{2})\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}}\Biggl(1+\Bigl(1-\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigl(\sum_{e\geq 1}\Bigl(\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}}^{e})}{N({\mathfrak{P}}^{e})}-\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}}^{e+1})}{N({\mathfrak{P}}^{e+1})}\Bigr)\Bigr)\Biggr)\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{K}\log(1+\eta_{2})\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}}\Bigl(1-\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)^{-1}\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)\prod_{{\mathfrak{P}}}\Biggl(\Bigl(1-\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Bigr)+\frac{\rho({\mathfrak{P}})}{N({\mathfrak{P}})}\Biggr)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{K}\frac{\log(1+\eta_{2})}{\tilde{\mathfrak{S}}}.

(10.17)

Putting together (
10.15
) (
10.16
) and (
10.17
) we see that

\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}Z_{1}({\mathfrak{I}})Z_{2}({\mathfrak{I}})=\frac{g((m^{\prime}))(\log{1+\eta_{2}})c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/m_{0}I)}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}+O(\eta_{2}^{2}I).

(10.18)

Finally, we recall the definition (
10.12
) of
n_{p}
. Since
f
is the product of two linear factors when
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})
, we have
n_{p}=2+O(1/p)
for all
p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]
. Thus

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\frac{n_{p}}{p}=\frac{(2+O(\eta_{2}))\log(P_{2}/P_{1})}{D_{f}}.

(10.19)

Putting together (
10.8
), (
10.9
), (
10.18
) and (
10.19
) now gives the result.
∎

We are now in a position to establish Proposition
9.13
.

Proof of Proposition
9.13
.

We see that putting together Lemma
10.1
and
10.2
gives

T_{sieve}=(2+O(\eta_{2}^{1/3}))\eta_{2}|\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(X_{0})|c_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}(X_{0}^{4}/mI)\frac{g(m^{\prime})}{m^{\prime}{}^{3}}\frac{\log(P_{2}/P_{1})}{\varphi(D_{f})}.

provided
B^{4}>X^{1+\epsilon}RP_{2}^{12}
. Recalling that
R=X^{\epsilon_{00}}
,
P_{2}=X^{\tau^{\prime}}
,
B^{4}=X^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}t_{i}}\geq X^{\sum_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\theta_{i}}
we see that this condition is satisfied provided

\sum_{i=1}^{\ell^{\prime}}\theta_{i}>1+\epsilon_{00}+12\tau^{\prime}

and
\epsilon
is taken sufficently small. This gives the result.
∎

11
Proposition
9.14
: The term
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

In this section we use the dispersion method to bound
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})
and establish Proposition
9.14
. Let us recall the expression of
T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})

T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{b}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathbf{v}}_{0},m^{\prime},p)}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})-\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})).

To simplify some notation we will write

\tilde{g}({\mathfrak{b}}):=\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})-\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}}).

(11.1)

We first split the sum over
{\mathfrak{b}}
into ideal classes
{\mathcal{C}}\in Cl_{K}
. Let
{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}=(N({\mathfrak{c}})/{\mathfrak{c}})
. Since the ideals in the set
{\mathcal{A}}
are principal, the ideals
{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathbf{c}}
and
{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
are principal. Therefore they are respectively of the form

(\alpha),(\beta)
with
{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}|(\alpha)
,
{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}|(\beta)
with
V\alpha=a_{1}\nu_{1}+a_{2}\nu_{2}+a_{3}\nu_{3}+a_{4}\nu_{4}
,
V\beta=b_{1}\nu_{1}+b_{2}\nu_{2}+b_{3}\nu_{3}+b_{3}\nu_{4}
,
where
a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}
and with
{\mathbf{a}},{\mathbf{b}}
lying in the fundamental domain
{\mathcal{D}}
. We will write
{\mathbf{a}}=(a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4})
,
{\mathbf{b}}=(b_{1},b_{2},b_{3},b_{4})
.
In order to handle the modulo
m
condition between
{\mathfrak{b}}
and
{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{a}}
we plit the sums according to some congruence classes on
\alpha
,
\beta
modulo
m^{\prime}
. Together this gives

T_{1}({\mathcal{R}})=\sum_{{\mathcal{C}}\in Cl_{K}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{a}}_{0},{\mathbf{b}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})}\\
{N({\mathfrak{c}})({\mathbf{a}}_{0}\diamond{\mathbf{b}}_{0})_{i}\equiv({\mathbf{v}}_{0})_{i}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime}),\ {\rm for}\ i=1,2,3,4}\end{subarray}}\tilde{T}_{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathcal{R}},{\mathbf{a}}_{0},{\mathbf{b}}_{0}),

(11.2)

with
(a_{0})_{4}=0
since
({\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}})_{4}=0
and

{\mathfrak{c}}\in{\mathcal{C}}
is a well chosen representant, and
{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}
as above

\tilde{T}_{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathcal{R}},{\mathbf{a}}_{0},{\mathbf{b}}_{0})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{\mathbf{a}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})}\\
{{\mathbf{b}}\equiv{\mathbf{b}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})}\\
{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}|(\alpha),{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}|(\beta)}\\
{(\alpha\beta)/(N({\mathfrak{c}}))\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(p)}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}(\frac{(\alpha)}{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}})\tilde{g}((\beta)/{\mathfrak{c}})),

with now
\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(p)=\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathbf{0}},1,p)
.

We recall that our previous conditions imply that there exists
A
,
B
such that

N({\mathfrak{a}})\in[A^{4},2A^{4}]
,
N({\mathfrak{b}})\in[B^{4},2B^{4}]
.

We will use the notation of

[
14
, p. 80 and 71]
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:x_{i}\in[X_{i},X_{i}(1+\eta_{1})],i=1,2,3,\ x_{4}=0,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad N(\sum_{i=1}^{3}x_{i}\nu_{i})\in[X_{0}^{4},X_{0}^{4}(1+\eta_{2})]\Bigr\},

(11.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\Bigl\{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:\|a\|\in[A,2A],{\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}}_{1}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}},\ {\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}}_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}}\Bigr\}.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be a fundamental domain such that if
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}({\mathfrak{a}})=1
and
{\mathfrak{a}}{\mathfrak{I}}=V^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{4}a_{i}\nu_{i}
then if
\alpha\in{\mathcal{F}}
,
a_{i}\ll A
and similarly,
b_{i}\ll B
for all
1\leq i\leq 4
.

We will concentrate on ideals
{\mathfrak{b}}
with not too many divisors. For this we introduce a slight variant of
\tilde{g}

g_{\mathbf{b}}=\begin{cases}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})-\tilde{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{2}}({\mathfrak{b}})&\text{if}\ \tau({\mathfrak{b}})\leq\varepsilon_{0}^{-2},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Following
[
14
, section 11]
except that we apply Lemma
9.8
, we prove that we can replace
\tilde{g}_{\mathbf{b}}
by
g_{\mathbf{b}}
with a error term less

O(\varepsilon_{0}^{2}X_{0}^{3}(\log X)^{O(1)})
.
This modification will permit us to bound the terms
g_{\mathbf{b}}
by
O(\varepsilon^{-2})
.

Thus now we have to concentrate on sums

T_{\mathfrak{c}}({\mathcal{R}},{\mathbf{a}}_{0},{\mathbf{b}}_{0})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\\
{p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{a}}\equiv{\mathbf{a}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})}\\
{{\mathbf{b}}\equiv{\mathbf{b}}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})}\\
{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}|(\alpha),{\mathfrak{c}}^{\prime}|(\beta)}\\
{{\mathfrak{I}}(\alpha\beta)/(N({\mathfrak{c}}))\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(p)}\\
{\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{F}}({\mathfrak{a}})\mathbf{1}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{1}}(\frac{(\alpha)}{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}})g_{(\beta)/{\mathfrak{c}}}.

(11.4)

11.1
Cosmetic reductions

For
T>0
, we denote by

{\mathcal{C}}_{T}
the subset of
\mathbb{R}^{4}
defined by

{\mathcal{C}}_{T}=\{{\mathbf{a}}\in\mathbb{R}^{4}:N({\mathfrak{a}})\in[T^{4},T^{4}(1+\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2})]\}.

By Weber’s Theorem
[
19
]
, we have

|{\mathcal{C}}_{T}|=\lambda_{K}T^{4}\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}+O(T^{3}),

for some
\lambda_{K}
depending only on
K
.
It will make some later technicalities simpler if we introduce the restriction
p\nmid N(\mathbf{b})
to the terms in
T_{1}
. By Proposition
9.11
and the divisor bound, we can do this at the cost of an error term of size

\ll\sum_{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap C_{B}}\\
{N({\mathfrak{b}})\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathfrak{b}}{\mathfrak{u}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(0,1,p)}\end{subarray}}X^{\varepsilon}\ll X^{\varepsilon}(X^{3}P_{1}^{-1}+X^{2}B^{4/3}P_{2}^{2}+B^{4}P_{2}^{5}).

This is acceptably small provided

B<\frac{X^{3/4-\epsilon}}{P_{2}^{3/2}}.

(11.5)

When
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})
, the function
f\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
factors as the product of two linear factors. Thus the condition
p|f(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})
is equivalent to
p|\mathbf{v}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})
or
p|\mathbf{w}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})
for two non-zero vectors
\mathbf{v}_{p},\mathbf{w}_{p}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
. There are
O(p^{5})
choices of
\mathbf{a}_{p},\mathbf{b}_{p}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
(\mathbf{a}_{p}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{p})={\mathbf{v}}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}_{p}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{p})=w_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}_{p}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{p})
whenever
p
is sufficiently large in terms of
f
. Therefore, as above, provided (
11.5
) holds, the contribution of the
\mathbf{a}
,
\mathbf{b}
such that

p|\mathbf{v}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})
and
p|\mathbf{w}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})
is bounded by

X^{\varepsilon}\sum_{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathbf{a}}_{p},{\mathbf{b}}_{p}\in\{1,\ldots,p\}^{4}\\
{({\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}})_{4}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\\
{p|{\mathbf{v}}_{p}\cdot({\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}})}\\
{p|{\mathbf{w}}_{p}\cdot({\mathbf{a}}\diamond{\mathbf{b}})}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathbf{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap C_{B}}\\
{{\mathbf{b}}\equiv{\mathbf{b}}_{p}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{{\mathfrak{u}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(0,1,p)}\\
{{\mathfrak{b}}|{\mathfrak{u}}}\\
{{\mathbf{u}}\equiv({\mathbf{a}}_{p}\diamond{\mathbf{b}}_{p})\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\end{subarray}}1\ll X^{3+\varepsilon}P_{1}^{-1}.

Putting this together, we see that it suffices for us to estimate for each
C\in Cl_{K}
and each
\mathbf{a}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})
the sums

T_{3}(\mathcal{R}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{{\mathfrak{I}}\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p\nmid N(\mathbf{b})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})\in{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}}\\
p|\mathbf{v}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})\\
\mathbf{a}\equiv\mathbf{a}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
{{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}|{\mathfrak{a}}}\\
{\mathfrak{a}}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(p)\end{subarray}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{R}_{1}}(\frac{{\mathfrak{a}}}{{\mathfrak{M}}_{0}{\mathfrak{I}}{\mathfrak{c}}})g_{(\beta)/{\mathfrak{c}}}.

(11.6)

11.2
Dispersion method

We swap the order of summation, and apply Cauchy-Schwarz. The ideals
{\mathfrak{I}}
and
{\mathfrak{a}}/{\mathfrak{I}}
are coprime since
N({\mathfrak{I}})<X^{\theta_{i}}
for all
1\leq i\leq\ell
.
In the application of Cauchy-Schwarz we can group these ideals together. We recall that the set
{\mathcal{R}}_{X}

is defined in (
4.5
).
This gives

T_{3}^{2}\leq\eta^{4}A^{4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
\mathbf{a}\equiv\mathbf{a}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{X_{0}}\\
p|\mathbf{v}_{p}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b})\\
\mathbf{b}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p\nmid N(\mathbf{b})\end{subarray}}g_{\mathbf{b}}\Bigr)^{2}.

Thus we see that

\displaystyle T_{3}^{2}\ll\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{2}A^{4}T_{4}

(11.7)

where, with the notation (
11.3
)

T_{4}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
\mathbf{a}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\\
p_{1}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})\\
p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}1.

Thus we wish to show that
T_{4}=o(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{6}A^{2}B^{6})
.

11.3
Collinear
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}

We separate the situation when
\mathbf{b}_{1}
and
\mathbf{b}_{2}
are collinear (in which case we have
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})=0
where
\wedge(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
is the
L^{2}
norm of the six
2\times 2
subdeterminants of the
2\times 4
matrix with columns
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{y}
. Thus we have

T_{4}=T_{5}+T_{6},

(11.8)

where
T_{5}
is those terms with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})=0
and
T_{6}
is those terms with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0
.

We first concentrate on
T_{5}
.

Lemma 11.1
.

T_{5}\ll A^{3}B^{3}(\log{X})^{O(1)}.

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{c}
be the shortest non-zero vector with integer components which is collinear with
\mathbf{b}_{1}
(this is
\mathbf{b}_{1}
divided by the
\gcd
of its components). Then we see that
\mathbf{b}_{1}=\lambda_{1}\mathbf{c}
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}
, and since
\mathbf{b}_{2}
is collinear with
\mathbf{b}_{1}
, we also have that
\mathbf{b}_{2}=\lambda_{2}\mathbf{c}
for some
\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus we see that

T_{5}\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\\
\|\mathbf{c}\|\ll B\end{subarray}}\sum_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\ll B/\|\mathbf{c}\|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})_{4}=0\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}|f(\lambda_{1}\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})\\
p_{2}|f(\lambda_{2}\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})\end{subarray}}1.

We see that the inner sum is
O(1)
since
P_{1}\gg B^{\epsilon}
and
f(\lambda_{1}\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})\ll B^{O(1)}
. We then split the size of
\|\mathbf{c}\|
into dyadic ranges, giving

T_{5}\ll(\log{X})\sup_{C\ll B}\frac{B^{2}}{C^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{c}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\\
\|\mathbf{c}\|\asymp C\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})_{4}=0\end{subarray}}1.

We now let
\mathbf{z}=(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c})
. By the divisor bound, given
\mathbf{z}
there are
O(\tau_{K}(\mathfrak{z}))
choices of
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{c}
. Thus we see that

T_{5}\ll(\log{X})\sup_{C\ll B}\frac{B^{2}}{C^{2}}\sum_{z_{1},z_{2},z_{3}\ll AC}\tau_{K}(z_{1}\nu_{1}+z_{2}\nu_{2}+z_{3}\nu_{3})\ll A^{3}B^{3}(\log{X})^{O(1)}.

∎

Thus we are left to bound
T_{6}
.

11.4
Lattice counts

We now concentrate on the inner sum. Let
\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}
denote the lattices

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}:\,(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})_{4}=(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})_{4}=0\},

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}

\displaystyle:=\{\mathbf{x}\in\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}:\,p_{1}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})\}.

Thus the inner sum in
T_{6}
is

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}\cap\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}\\
{\mathbf{a}}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\\
\end{subarray}}1.

If
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
are not collinear, then
\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
is a lattice of rank 2, and so it has a Minkowski-reduced basis
\{\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2}\}
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\leq\|\mathbf{z}_{2}\|
. Thus we have that

\sum_{\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}\cap\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}}1=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}\\
\lambda_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathbf{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}c_{1}+\lambda_{2}c_{2}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})\\
\lambda_{1}c_{3}+\lambda_{2}c_{4}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{2})\end{subarray}}1,

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}
depending only on
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
,
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
. The condition
\lambda_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathbf{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{A}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}
forces
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
to lie in a region
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{4}
. Since
\|\lambda_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathbf{z}_{2}\|\asymp|\lambda_{1}|\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|+|\lambda_{2}|\|\mathbf{z}_{2}\|
and
\mathcal{C}_{A}
only contains vectors of norm
O(A)
, we see that lying in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
forces
\lambda_{1}\ll A/\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|
and
\lambda_{2}\ll A/\|\mathbf{z}_{2}\|
, so
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
has volume
O(A^{2}/\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\|\mathbf{z}_{2}\|)
.

By Davenport’s Theorem on counting lattice points (
[
14
, Lemma 7.1]
for example), we have that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}\\
\lambda_{1}c_{1}+\lambda_{2}c_{2}=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ [p_{1},p_{2}])\end{subarray}}1=\frac{{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}}+O\Bigl(\frac{A}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigr)

where
f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}=[\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}:\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}]
is the index of the lattices, given explicitly in terms of
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4},p_{1},p_{2}
by

f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}=\begin{cases}1,\qquad&c_{1}\equiv c_{2}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})\text{ and }c_{3}\equiv c_{4}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{2}),\\
p_{2},&c_{1}\equiv c_{2}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})\text{ and }c_{3},c_{4}\text{ not both }0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{2}),\\
p_{1},&c_{3}\equiv c_{4}\equiv 0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{2})\text{ and }c_{1},c_{2}\text{ not both }0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1}),\\
p_{1},&p_{1}=p_{2}\text{ and }c_{1}c_{4}\equiv c_{2}c_{3}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})\text{ and }c_{1},c_{2}\text{ not all }0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1}),\\
p_{1}p_{2},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

We split
T_{6}
into the contribution from the main term
{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})/f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}
and the error term
O(A/\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|)
. This gives

T_{6}=T_{8}+O(T_{7}),

(11.9)

where

\displaystyle T_{7}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{A}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|},

\displaystyle T_{8}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}}.

We first show that the contribution
T_{7}
from the error term is small.

Lemma 11.2
.

T_{7}\ll AB^{7}P_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

We note that
\mathbf{z}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
with
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|^{2}\leq\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\cdot\|\mathbf{z}_{2}\|\ll{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})\ll B^{2}
. Thus
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\ll B
. Thus we can rearrange the summation to give

T_{7}\ll P_{2}^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}}\frac{A}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\ll AP_{2}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\\
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\ll B\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigl(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
(\mathbf{b}\diamond\mathbf{z}_{1})_{4}=0\end{subarray}}1\Bigr)^{2}

The condition
(\mathbf{b}\diamond\mathbf{z}_{1})_{4}=0
forces
\mathbf{b}
to lie in a rank 3 lattice of determinant
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|
. Thus the inner sum is
O(B^{3}/\|z_{1}\|+B^{2})
. Thus we obtain the bound

T_{7}\ll AP_{2}^{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\\
\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|\ll B\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\frac{B^{6}}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|^{3}}+\frac{B^{4}}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigr)\ll AB^{7}P_{2}^{2}.

This gives the result.
∎

Thus we are left to show that

T_{8}=o(\eta_{\mathcal{X}}^{4}A^{2}B^{6}).

11.5
Further lattice estimates

We recall that
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
is the region
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
\lambda_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}+\lambda_{2}\mathbf{z}_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{B}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}
. We see that this has volume
{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})/{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
, where
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
is the determinant of the lattice (that is, the 2-dimensional area of parallelogram generated by
\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2}
) and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
is the 2-dimensional region formed by intersecting
\mathcal{C}_{B}
with the
\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2}
plane

T_{8}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}.

We first establish a few simple estimates.

Lemma 11.3
.

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\frac{{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}}\ll{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})+O\Bigl(\frac{AP_{2}^{2}}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigr).

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
\mathbf{a}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\\
p_{1}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1}\\
p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2}\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
\mathbf{a}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\\
p_{1}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1}\\
p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2}\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
(\mathbf{a}\in{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}=0\end{subarray}}1

\displaystyle\ll{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})+O\Bigl(\frac{A}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigr).

On the other hand, we know that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{A}\\
\mathbf{a}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{{\mathbf{b}}_{1},{\mathbf{b}}_{2}}\\
p_{1}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1}\\
p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2}\end{subarray}}1=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\Bigl(\frac{{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}}+O\Bigl(\frac{A}{\|\mathbf{z}_{1}\|}\Bigr)\Bigr).

Putting these together gives the result.
∎

Lemma 11.4
.

Let

\mathcal{C}_{C,d;\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}:=\#\Bigl\{\mathbf{b}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}:\,\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim\frac{B^{2}}{C},\,\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)\Bigr\}.

Then we have

\#\mathcal{C}_{C,d;\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}}\ll\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{d}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{Cd}\Bigr)^{3}.

Proof.

The condition
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
forces
\mathbf{b}_{1}
to lie in a cylinder
\mathcal{C}
with axis of length
O(B)
proportional to
\mathbf{b}_{2}
, and with radius
O(B/C)
. We then see that we can cover this cylinder with

\ll\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{d}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{Cd}\Bigr)^{3}

different hypercubes
\mathcal{B}
of side length
d
. Finally, there is at most one choice of
\mathbf{b}_{1}
in a hypercube
\mathcal{B}
of side length
d
which satisfies
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)
, which gives the result.
∎

For any
\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
, the notation
\mathbf{c}_{1}\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}
indicates that the two vectors are proportional.

Lemma 11.5
.

If
\mathbf{c}_{1}\not\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
then

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\,\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
\mathrm{primitive}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}\ll\frac{B^{6}}{p^{8}}+B^{17/3}.

Proof.

We recall that
\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
is the lattice in
\mathbb{Z}^{4}
of
\mathbf{x}
with
(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})_{4}=(\mathbf{x}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})_{4}=0
. By
[
14
, Lemma 10.1]
, this has determinant
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})/D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
, where
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})
is the
L^{2}
norm of the six
2\times 2
subdeterminants of the matrix with columns
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
, and
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
is the greatest common divisor of these six subdeterminants. Note that this implies
\mathbf{b}_{1}\propto\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
, so since
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
are primitive we must have
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}\leq B
when
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0
.

We consider separately those
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\ll B
, those with
B\ll\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\ll B^{4/3}
, and those
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
with

D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d,\qquad\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C

for each
1\leq d\leq B
and
1\leq C\leq B^{2/3}
with
C
running through powers of
2
.

If
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\ll B
then
\mathbf{b}_{1}
lies within
O(1)
of the line proportional to
\mathbf{b}_{2}
, and so there are
O(B)
choices of
\mathbf{b}_{1}
. Since
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})\geq 1
, these terms contribute a total (ignoring the congruence conditions
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
for an upper bound)

\ll\sum_{\|\mathbf{b}_{2}\|\ll B}O(B)\ll B^{5}.

If
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\in[B,B^{4/3}]
then we separately consider those with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
for
C\in[B^{2/3},B]
running through powers of 2, and again drop the congruence constraints. By Lemma
11.4
there are

\ll B\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{C}\Bigr)^{3}\ll\frac{B^{4}}{C^{3}}

choices of
\mathbf{b}_{1}
given
\mathbf{b}_{2}
. If
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
then
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})\geq B/C
(since
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1}\mathbf{b}_{2}}\leq B
). Thus these terms contribute

\ll\sum_{C=2^{j}\in[B^{2/3},B]}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\end{subarray}}\frac{C}{B}\frac{B^{4}}{C^{3}}\ll B^{17/3}.

Thus we are left to consider the terms with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
for some
C\leq B^{2/3}
. The condition
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
forces
\mathbf{b}_{1}\propto\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)
, and so
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\lambda\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)
for some
\lambda\in\{1,\dots,d\}
. Since
\mathbf{c}_{1}\not\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, we see
p\nmid d
. Thus
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{0}(\lambda)\ (\mathrm{mod}\ dp)
, where
\mathbf{c}_{0}(\lambda)\equiv\lambda\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)
and
\mathbf{c}_{0}(\lambda)\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. By Lemma
11.4
, the number of choices of
\mathbf{b}_{1}
is therefore

\ll\sum_{1\leq\lambda\leq d}\#\mathcal{C}_{C,pd,\mathbf{c}_{0}(\lambda),\mathbf{b}_{2}}\ll d\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{pd}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{pCd}\Bigr)^{3}\ll B+\frac{B^{4}}{p^{4}C^{3}d^{3}}.

If
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
and
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
then
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})\gg B^{2}/(Cd)
. Thus we find that the contribution from terms with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\geq B^{4/3}
is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\,\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\geq B\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
\mathrm{primitive}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{1\leq d\leq B}\sum_{C=2^{j}\ll B^{2/3}}\frac{dC}{B^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\Bigl(B+\frac{B^{4}}{C^{3}d^{3}p^{4}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{B^{6}}{p^{8}}+B^{17/3}.

Thus we have a suitable bound in each case, giving the result.
∎

Lemma 11.6
.

Let
\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}
be non-zero
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
with
\mathbf{c}_{1}\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Then we have

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\,\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
\mathrm{primitive}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\\
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}\ll\frac{B^{6}}{p^{7}}+B^{17/3}.

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of Lemma
11.5
. Since the estimates in the proof of Lemma
11.5
when
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\ll B^{4/3}
didn’t depend on whether
p|D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
or not, an identical argument shows that the contribution of
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\ll B^{4/3}
contributes
O(B^{17/3})
. Therefore we just need to consider the contribution when
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\gg B^{4/3}
.

We split the summation according to
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
and
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
. Since
\mathbf{c}_{1}\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, we have
\mathbf{c}_{1}\equiv\lambda_{0}\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
for some
\lambda_{0}
. Since
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
and
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\mathrm{mod}\ p
we then see that
p|d
. The condition
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
forces
\mathbf{b}_{1}=\lambda\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ d)
for some
\lambda
, with
\lambda\equiv\lambda_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Thus, by Lemma
11.4
, the number of choices of
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
and
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
s

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\lambda\leq d\\
\lambda\equiv\lambda_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\end{subarray}}\#\mathcal{C}_{C,d,\lambda\mathbf{b}_{2},\mathbf{b}_{2}}

\displaystyle\ll\Bigl(1+\frac{B^{4}}{p^{4}}\Bigr)\frac{d}{p}\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{d}\Bigr)\Bigl(1+\frac{B}{Cd}\Bigr)^{3}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{B^{8}}{p^{5}C^{3}d^{3}}+B^{5}.

When
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\sim B^{2}/C
and
D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}=d
we have
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})\gg B^{2}/(Cd)
. Thus the total contribution from terms with
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\gg B^{4/3}
is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d\leq B\\
p|d\end{subarray}}\sum_{C=2^{j}\ll B^{2/3}}\frac{Cd}{B^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{B^{8}}{p^{5}C^{3}d^{3}}+B^{5}\Bigr)\ll\frac{B^{6}}{p^{7}}+B^{17/3}.

This gives the result.
∎

We are now able to make progress on our aim of bounding
T_{8}
.

Lemma 11.7
.

Let
T_{8}
be as given by (
11.9
). Then we have

T_{8}\ll\eta_{2}A^{2}B^{6}+\eta_{2}^{-4}A^{2}\sup_{{\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}}\Bigl(|T_{11}|+|T_{12}|\Bigr),

where the supremum is over all hypercubers
{\mathcal{C}}_{1},{\mathcal{C}}_{2}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{B}
of side length
\eta_{2}B
and

\displaystyle T_{11}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{2}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
p\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1})N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p,p}{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})},

(11.10)

\displaystyle T_{12}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{p_{1}p_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{2}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}}{\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})}.

(11.11)

Proof.

We wish to replace
\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
with a quantity which doesn’t depend on
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
by splitting
\mathcal{C}_{B}
into
O(\eta_{2}^{-4}\eta_{1}^{4})
smaller hypercubes of side length
\eta_{2}B
. We see that
{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
depends continuously on the components of
\mathbf{b}_{1}
and
\mathbf{b}_{2}
, and that
{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
is always of size
O(A^{2})
. Moreover, if we restrict
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
to hypercubes of side length
\eta_{2}B
then
{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})
varies by
O(\eta_{2}A^{2})
as
\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}
vary within these hypercubes. Thus we see that

\displaystyle T_{8}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}

\displaystyle\ll T_{9}+\eta_{2}^{-4}A^{2}\sup_{\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}}|T_{10}|,

(11.12)

where

\displaystyle T_{9}

\displaystyle:=\eta_{2}\varepsilon_{0}^{-4}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{B}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\end{subarray}}\frac{{\mathrm{\,vol\,}}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})},

\displaystyle T_{10}

\displaystyle=T_{10}(\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},p_{2}\in[P_{1},P_{2}]\\
p_{1}\equiv p_{2}\equiv 1\ (\mathrm{mod}\ D_{f})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{b}_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{4}\cap\mathcal{C}_{2}\\
\mathbf{b}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{b}_{0}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ m^{\prime})\\
\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})\neq 0\\
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1}),\,p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})\end{subarray}}\frac{g_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}g_{\mathbf{b}_{2}}}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})}.

By the above lemmas, we have that

T_{9}\ll\varepsilon^{-4}\eta_{2}A^{2}B^{6},

which is acceptable if
\eta_{2}\ll\eta_{1}^{9}
. Thus we are left to bound
T_{10}
. We separate the terms when the two primes in the outer sum are the same. Thus

T_{10}=T_{11}+T_{12},

(11.13)

where
T_{11}
denotes the terms with
p_{1}=p_{2}
and
T_{12}
those terms with
p_{1}\neq p_{2}
.

T_{11}
clearly is equal to the expression given in the lemma, but (recalling that
{\mathrm{\,det\,}}(\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}})=\wedge(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})/D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
) we need to show that
f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}=p_{1}p_{2}
in
T_{12}
to obtain the desired expression. We first note that since
p_{1}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{1})
the multiplication-by-
\mathbf{b}_{1}
matrix
M_{\mathbf{b}_{1}}
is invertible
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
. This means that for every
\mathbf{x}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
there is a unique
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
such that
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
, and so
\mathbf{v}_{p_{1}}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
is therefore a non-trivial constraint on the components of
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{1})
. Similarly since
p_{2}\nmid N(\mathbf{b}_{2})
, we see
p_{2}|\mathbf{v}_{p_{2}}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})
is a non-trivial constraints on the components of
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p_{2})
. From this it follows that we have that
f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p_{1},p_{2}}=p_{1}p_{2}
, and so
T_{12}
is given by the expression in the lemma.
∎

First we concentrate on
T_{11}
.

11.6
The case
p_{1}=p_{2}

In this section we wish to bound the sum
T_{11}
from (
11.10
). We first see by Lemma
11.6
the contribution of terms with
\mathbf{b}_{1}\propto\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
to
T_{11}
is

\displaystyle\ll\sum_{p\in[P_{1},P_{2}]}\sum_{\mathbf{c}_{1}\propto\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\Bigl(\frac{B^{6}}{p^{7}}+B^{17/3}\Bigr)\ll\frac{B^{6}}{P_{1}}+P_{2}^{6}B^{17/3}.

Thus we have

T_{11}=T_{11}^{\prime}+O\Bigl(\frac{B^{6}}{P_{1}}+P_{2}^{6}B^{17/3}\Bigr),

where
T_{11}^{\prime}
counts those terms in
T_{11}
with
\mathbf{b}_{1}\not\propto\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, or equivalently with
p\nmid D_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}
.

When
\mathbf{b}_{1}\not\propto\mathbf{b}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, we see that the constraints
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})_{4}=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
and
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})_{4}=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
are two linearly independent linear constraints on
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. In particular, the index
f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p,p}=[\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}}:\Lambda_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p,p}]
simplifies to give

\frac{1}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p,p}}=\frac{\#\{\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p):\,(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})_{4}=(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})_{4}=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)\}}{p^{2}}.

We separate the above count according to the rank of the multiplication-by-
\mathbf{a}
matrix
M_{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Thus

\frac{1}{f_{\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2},p,p}}=\sum_{i=0}^{4}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\widetilde{S}_{i}(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2}),

(11.14)

where
\widetilde{S}_{i}(\mathbf{b}_{1},\mathbf{b}_{2})
counts those
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
M_{\mathbf{a}}
has rank
i
and satisfies
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})_{4}=(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})_{4}=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{1})=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{b}_{2})=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
.

First we consider
\widetilde{S}_{4}
.

Lemma 11.8
.

\sum_{\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\widetilde{S}_{4}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})\ll p^{6}.

Proof.

In this case
M_{\mathbf{a}}
has rank 4, and so is invertible
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Given any choice of
\mathbf{c}_{1}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
with
p\nmid N(\mathbf{c}_{1})
, we see that
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1}=M_{\mathbf{c}_{1}}\mathbf{a}
where the multiplication-by-
\mathbf{c}_{1}
matrix
M_{\mathbf{c}_{1}}
has determinant
N(\mathbf{c}_{1})
, and so is invertible
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Therefore, given any choice of
\mathbf{x}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, there is a unique choice of
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
with
p\nmid N(\mathbf{a})
such that
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{x}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Similarly, since we only consider
\mathbf{a}
with
M_{\mathbf{a}}
is invertible, given any choice of
\mathbf{y}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
there is then a unique choice of
\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{y}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Since there are
O(p^{4})
choices of
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
with
\mathbf{x}_{4}=\mathbf{y}_{4}=0
and
\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{y}=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, there are therefore
O(p^{4})
choices of
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
p\nmid N(\mathbf{a})
and
(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1})_{4}=(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{2})_{4}=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1})=\mathbf{v}\cdot(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{2})=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Thus we have that

\sum_{\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\widetilde{S}_{4}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})\ll p^{6},

as required.
∎

Now we consider
\widetilde{S}_{2}
and
\widetilde{S}_{3}
.

Lemma 11.9
.

\sum_{\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)}\frac{1}{p^{2}}\Bigl(\widetilde{S}_{2}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})+\widetilde{S}_{3}(\mathbf{c}_{1},\mathbf{c}_{2})\Bigr)\ll p^{6}.

Proof.

Since
M_{\mathbf{a}}
is not invertible
\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
and has determinant
N(\mathbf{a})
, we see that
p|N(\mathbf{a})
and so
p|N(\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1})=N(\mathbf{a})N(\mathbf{c}_{1})
. Since
f(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})
is an irreducible polynomial which splits into two linear factors over a quadratic extension, and
N(x_{1}\nu_{1}+x_{2}\nu_{2}+x_{3}\nu_{3})
is a quartic irreducible polynomial which has no linear factors over any quadratic extension, these polynomials have no common polynomial factors over a mutual splitting field, and so define an algebraic variety of codimension 2. Thus (by Hilbert’s Theorem 90 and the Lang-Weil bound) there are
O(p)
choices of
(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
f(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})=N(x_{1}\nu_{1}+x_{2}\nu_{2}+x_{3}\nu_{3})=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Thus there are
O(p^{2})
choices of
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
with
p|N(\mathbf{x}),N(\mathbf{y})
and
x_{4}=y_{4}=\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{v}\cdot\mathbf{y}=0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
. Given
\mathbf{c}_{1}
with
p\nmid N(\mathbf{c}_{1})
and
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{y}
as above, here is a unique
\mathbf{a}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
such that
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{1}\equiv\mathbf{x}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
, and there are
O(p^{2})
choices of
\mathbf{c}_{2}
such that
\mathbf{a}\diamond\mathbf{c}_{2}\equiv\mathbf{y}\ (\mathrm{mod}\ p)
provided
M_{\mathbf{a}}
has rank
2
or
3
